Typing one into any document under `docs/` fails `npm run gtm:check`.\n21\t13. To learn what an application actually has, run `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` rather than assembling it from source and prose (`docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`). Read `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]` — every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim. It reads checked-in source only: it opens no database, contacts no provider, and reports no runtime or authorization state.\n22\t14. **Compatibility Backfill Rule.** When you add or change a *horizontal* capability — one every domain could use, such as the package seam, a declared capability contract, module evolution, an evidence discipline or an agent-facing surface — record every existing domain's status against it in `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` in the same PR, using `aligned | partial | deferred | not_applicable | needs_extraction` with a one-line reason. Declaring the gap is required; closing it in the same PR is not. Do **not** refactor a legacy domain to close a row: extraction is sequenced work (`docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md`), not something a feature PR does on the way past.\n23\t15. A Solution Plan is a **checked file with a contract**, not prose with headings (`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md`). Write it, record the `app inspect` report it was written against, and run `npm run crm -- solution check <plan.json>` before writing code and again before the review — a plan bound to a composition that has since moved reports `PLAN_STALE`. A plan never carries a command: nothing in this framework executes one, and the validator refuses it.\n24\t16. **Parallel coding agents.** Each agent works in its own sibling worktree **outside** the repository, owns exactly one branch, and one final integrator reconciles the shared truth. The three rules are in *Parallel coding agents* below.\n25\t\n26\t## Parallel coding agents\n27\t\n28\tSeveral agents may work on one milestone at once. Three rules, because breaking\n29\tany of them has already cost this repository a wave of published numbers:\n30\t\n31\t1. **One worktree per agent, outside the repository.** `git worktree add\n32\t   ../<repo>-worktrees/<name>` — a sibling directory, never a path inside the\n33\t   checkout, so no agent's build output, temporary database or generated site\n34\t   lands in another agent's tree.\n35\t2. **One branch owner per worktree.** The agent that owns a branch is the only\n36\t   one that commits to it. An agent that needs another agent's change waits for\n37\t   the merge or rebases onto it; it does not reach into a worktree it does not own.\n38\t3. **One final integrator reconciles shared truth.** Every wave ends with a\n39\t   single pass over the files every branch touches — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`,\n40\t   `site/claims.json`, the JTBD matrix, the roadmaps — because a merge that\n41\t   resolves a conflict in a *measured record* silently discards a measurement.\n42\t   That is not hypothetical: a branch re-measured `site/claims.json`, the merge\n43\t   kept main's older block, and the ledger ran a whole wave behind the suite with\n44\t   every gate green. `npm run gtm:check` now fails on that particular drift\n45\t   (`scripts/measurement.js`), and the integrator pass is what catches the rest.\n46\t\n47\tSequenced in `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §1.11.\n48\t\n49\t## Coding conventions\n50\t\n51\t- Node.js ESM and standard-library-first JavaScript.\n52\t- Use `// @ts-check` and JSDoc for public APIs.\n53\t- Prefer small explicit functions over hidden metaprogramming.\n54\t- Return domain objects, not raw SQLite rows with encoded JSON.\n55\t- Use cents for monetary values and ISO 4217 currency codes.\n56\t- Use ISO-8601 UTC timestamps.\n57\t- Log MCP diagnostics only to stderr; stdout is reserved for JSON-RPC.\n58\t\n59\t## Definition of done\n60\t\n61\t- behavior works end-to-end;\n62\t- tests cover happy path and policy boundary;\n63\t- trace and audit are visible;\n64\t- no direct table mutation outside module services;\n65\t- documentation is sufficient for another coding agent to continue;\n66\t- every claim in the docs, the ADR, the PR body and the JTBD matrix traces to a merged test — a capability and its limitation are stated in the same breath.\n67\t\n68\t## The DX Simplicity Gate\n69\t\n70\tBefore adding a new **agent-facing** command, tool, contract or namespace,\n71\tanswer these in the PR. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01AUmxEUswLeyhuP1x9evzLp","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Architecture\n2\t\n3\t## Core rule\n4\t\n5\t> CRM state is deterministic. AI agents interpret the user's intent and compose the system, but state changes pass through services and workflows.\n6\t\n7\t## Medusa-inspired mapping\n8\t\n9\t| Medusa concept | Accordo equivalent |\n10\t|---|---|\n11\t| Commerce module | CRM domain module |\n12\t| Product/customer/order | Offer/company/opportunity or contract |\n13\t| Workflow | Commercial process |\n14\t| Provider | Email, calendar, ERP or enrichment adapter |\n15\t| Admin extension | CRM workspace extension |\n16\t| Cloud CLI | Serve, migrate, inspect, trace and deploy commands |\n17\t| Docs MCP | Project schema, workflow and operational context |\n18\t| Agent skills | Repeatable module/workflow implementation instructions |\n19\t\n20\t## Layers\n21\t\n22\t```text\n23\tAdmin / API / SDK\n24\t        ↓\n25\tWorkflows and policies\n26\t        ↓\n27\tCRM module services\n28\t        ↓\n29\tSQLite adapter\n30\t\n31\tMCP + CLI sit beside the application and invoke the same public layer.\n32\tTrace + audit observe every controlled mutation.\n33\t```\n34\t\n35\t## Modules\n36\t\n37\tA module owns one domain boundary. It exposes a service and metadata. Other modules do not update its tables directly.\n38\t\n39\tCurrent modules:\n40\t\n41\t- Company\n42\t- Contact\n43\t- Opportunity\n44\t- Approval\n45\t\n46\t## Workflows\n47\t\n48\tA workflow is a named sequence of traced steps. It coordinates modules and providers. A step can optionally define compensation for reversible side effects.\n49\t\n50\tCurrent workflows:\n51\t\n52\t- `request-opportunity-stage-change`\n53\t- `decide-opportunity-approval`\n54\t\n55\t## Actions\n56\t\n57\tA record action is a lifecycle operation on a single record that is more than a field edit — qualify a lead, close an opportunity. Actions are code-first definitions (`packages/actions/generated/index.js`) exposed through one generic route, SDK method and Admin control rendered from metadata, never per-action generated files and never a declarative DSL.\n58\t\n59\tThe action runtime wraps `execute` in the guarantees a hand-rolled service method would not have: all business writes in one outer transaction (generated-service savepoints nest inside it), domain events held in a transaction-scoped outbox and dispatched only after commit, and a workflow trace written after the transaction resolves so a failed action still leaves a record. Fields a workflow owns are declared `writable: \"managed\"` in the manifest and refused by generic CRUD, so lifecycle state is enforced at the service boundary rather than in the UI.\n60\t\n61\tUse an action for a single-record lifecycle step; use a workflow for a multi-record or multi-step process, or when a human approval gate is involved. See `docs/ACTIONS.md`, ADR-011 and ADR-012.\n62\t\n63\t## Domain packages\n64\t\n65\tDomain behavior lives in optional packages built on the runtime's contracts, not in the core (ADR-018). A package declares itself as plain data — a name, its record manifests, its versioned policies, its actions and function-free schema metadata — and a project enables it with one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. The runtime validates the declaration, fingerprints each policy version (ADR-015), registers the actions and publishes the metadata under `/api/schema`; it never learns the domain's vocabulary. Removing the import removes the domain, and everything else keeps working.\n66\t\n67\t`packages/contracts/` (Milestone 12: contract activation and subscriptions) is the first one, and a third-party package uses the identical path. See `docs/CONTRACT_ACTIVATION.md` and the ADR-018 addendum.\n68\t\n69\t## Providers\n70\t\n71\tProviders isolate external systems from business logic. The proof of concept includes a notification provider contract and an in-memory implementation. Email, calendar, ERP and marketing providers belong here later.\n72\t\n73\t## MCP responsibilities\n74\t\n75\tMCP exposes:\n76\t\n77\t- project and CRM context as resources;\n78\t- controlled application operations as tools;\n79\t- reusable build/debug prompts.\n80\t\n81\tIt does not bypass services or workflows.\n82\t\n83\t## Extension rule\n84\t\n85\tA new business feature should normally add or change only:\n86\t\n87\t1. one module;\n88\t2. one workflow;\n89\t3. one API or MCP surface;\n90\t4. tests;\n91\t5. docs/decision record when architecture changes.\n92\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"4ecb5d60-fb99-4988-8f25-96c68a0114d8","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:15:41.755Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-01-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a1/fixture/ARCHITECTURE.md","content":"# Architecture\n\n## Core rule\n\n> CRM state is deterministic. AI agents interpret the user's intent and compose the system, but state changes pass through services and workflows.\n\n## Medusa-inspired mapping\n\n| Medusa concept | Accordo equivalent |\n|---|---|\n| Commerce module | CRM domain module |\n| Product/customer/order | Offer/company/opportunity or contract |\n| Workflow | Commercial process |\n| Provider | Email, calendar, ERP or enrichment adapter |\n| Admin extension | CRM workspace extension |\n| Cloud CLI | Serve, migrate, inspect, trace and deploy commands |\n| Docs MCP | Project schema, workflow and operational context |\n| Agent skills | Repeatable module/workflow implementation instructions |\n\n## Layers\n\n```text\nAdmin / API / SDK\n        ↓\nWorkflows and policies\n        ↓\nCRM module services\n        ↓\nSQLite adapter\n\nMCP + CLI sit beside the application and invoke the same public layer.\nTrace + audit observe every controlled mutation.\n```\n\n## Modules\n\nA module owns one domain boundary. It exposes a service and metadata. Other modules do not update its tables directly.\n\nCurrent modules:\n\n- Company\n- Contact\n- Opportunity\n- Approval\n\n## Workflows\n\nA workflow is a named sequence of traced steps. It coordinates modules and providers. A step can optionally define compensation for reversible side effects.\n\nCurrent workflows:\n\n- `request-opportunity-stage-change`\n- `decide-opportunity-approval`\n\n## Actions\n\nA record action is a lifecycle operation on a single record that is more than a field edit — qualify a lead, close an opportunity. Actions are code-first definitions (`packages/actions/generated/index.js`) exposed through one generic route, SDK method and Admin control rendered from metadata, never per-action generated files and never a declarative DSL.\n\nThe action runtime wraps `execute` in the guarantees a hand-rolled service method would not have: all business writes in one outer transaction (generated-service savepoints nest inside it), domain events held in a transaction-scoped outbox and dispatched only after commit, and a workflow trace written after the transaction resolves so a failed action still leaves a record. Fields a workflow owns are declared `writable: \"managed\"` in the manifest and refused by generic CRUD, so lifecycle state is enforced at the service boundary rather than in the UI.\n\nUse an action for a single-record lifecycle step; use a workflow for a multi-record or multi-step process, or when a human approval gate is involved. See `docs/ACTIONS.md`, ADR-011 and ADR-012.\n\n## Domain packages\n\nDomain behavior lives in optional packages built on the runtime's contracts, not in the core (ADR-018). A package declares itself as plain data — a name, its record manifests, its versioned policies, its actions and function-free schema metadata — and a project enables it with one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. The runtime validates the declaration, fingerprints each policy version (ADR-015), registers the actions and publishes the metadata under `/api/schema`; it never learns the domain's vocabulary. Removing the import removes the domain, and everything else keeps working.\n\n`packages/contracts/` (Milestone 12: contract activation and subscriptions) is the first one, and a third-party package uses the identical path. See `docs/CONTRACT_ACTIVATION.md` and the ADR-018 addendum.\n\n## Providers\n\nProviders isolate external systems from business logic. The proof of concept includes a notification provider contract and an in-memory implementation. Email, calendar, ERP and marketing providers belong here later.\n\n## MCP responsibilities\n\nMCP exposes:\n\n- project and CRM context as resources;\n- controlled application operations as tools;\n- reusable build/debug prompts.\n\nIt does not bypass services or workflows.\n\n## Extension rule\n\nA new business feature should normally add or change only:\n\n1. one module;\n2. one workflow;\n3. one API or MCP surface;\n4. tests;\n5. docs/decision record when architecture changes.\n","numLines":92,"startLine":1,"totalLines":92}}}

Volume and\n8\tgraduated tiering ARE supported.\n9\t\n10\t## The flow\n11\t\n12\t```text\n13\tdefine/sync catalog     provider call OUTSIDE the transaction → immutable products, versions, price books, offers, components, tiers\n14\t→ create Quote          one opportunity, one price book, one currency\n15\t→ add lines             one Offer + a quantity;   // requires actor.type === 'user'\n36\t```\n37\t\n38\tThe Admin adds a **Quotes** view (`#/quotes`, `#/quotes/<id>`) for browsing a\n39\tprice book, editing draft lines, submitting and deciding — every amount is\n40\tserver-calculated and every mutation is the same server action.\n41\t\n42\t## The pricing model\n43\t\n44\t```text\n45\tProduct → ProductVersion → Offer (rate plan) → PriceComponent(s) → Tier(s)\n46\t```\n47\t\n48\tAn **Offer** is the sellable package; it may carry several **PriceComponents**\n49\tmixing one-time and recurring charges. Each component declares:\n50\t\n51\t```text\n52\tchargeType     one_time | recurring\n53\tpricingModel   flat_fee | per_unit | volume | graduated\n54\tinterval       month | year        (null for one_time)\n55\tintervalCount  positive integer    (null for one_time)\n56\ttiers          ordered schedule    (volume/graduated only)\n57\tprovenance     provider, providerVersion, externalProductId,\n58\t               externalOfferId, externalPriceId, sourcePricingModel,\n59\t               sourceFingerprint\n60\t```\n61\t\n62\tA tier carries `upTo` (inclusive upper bound; `null` on the single final\n63\topen-ended tier), `unitAmountCents` and an explicit optional `flatAmountCents`.\n64\tSchedules must be strictly increasing and end open-ended, so they are gap-free\n65\tand overlap-free by construction.\n66\t\n67\tExample composite offer — **one quote line, one quantity**:\n68\t\n69\t```text\n70\tEnterprise Plan\n71\t  1. Setup and migration   one_time  + flat_fee   EUR 5,000.00\n72\t  2. Platform fee          recurring + flat_fee   EUR 2,000.00 / month\n73\t  3. Seats                 recurring + volume     1–20: EUR 50.00, 21–100: EUR 40.00, 101+: EUR 30.00 per seat/month\n74\t```\n75\t\n76\t### Quantity semantics (explicit, never silent)\n77\t\n78\t| Model | Line quantity behavior |\n79\t|---|---|\n80\t| `flat_fee` | charged **once** per line — quantity never multiplies it |\n81\t| `per_unit` | `unitAmount × quantity` |\n82\t| `volume` | the tier the **total** quantity reaches prices the **entire** quantity (+ that tier's flat amount once) |\n83\t| `graduated` | each band prices the quantity inside it (+ each receiving band's flat amount once) |\n84\t\n85\tAt 30 seats the example above bills setup once (not 30×), the platform fee\n86\tonce per month, and 30 × EUR 40.00 for seats (the whole quantity at the\n87\treached tier).\n88\t\n89\t### Provider mapping\n90\t\n91\t| Provider model | Normalized |\n92\t|---|---|\n93\t| Stripe one-time price | `one_time` |\n94\t| Stripe recurring price | `recurring` + interval/intervalCount |\n95\t| Stripe `tiers_mode: volume` | `volume` |\n96\t| Stripe `tiers_mode: graduated` | `graduated` |\n97\t| Zuora one-time charge | `one_time` |\n98\t| Zuora recurring charge | `recurring` |\n99\t| Zuora Volume Pricing | `volume` |\n100\t| Zuora Tiered/Cumulative Pricing | `graduated` |\n101\t| Stripe metered / Zuora usage | **unsupported** → `quoteEligible: false` |\n102\t\n103\t`sourcePricingModel` and the external ids keep provider provenance intact.\n104\t**No real Stripe/Zuora/ERP adapter or credential ships** — a deterministic\n105\tfixture provider proves the contract offline, and full Stripe or Zuora support\n106\tis not claimed.\n107\t\n108\t### Unsupported models fail closed\n109\t\n110\tMetered usage, overage, proration, ramp deals, minimum commitments,\n111\tattribute-based/dynamic pricing, tax-inclusive computation, FX and custom\n112\tprovider formulas are **never approximated as flat prices**. The offer is\n113\tstored with `quoteEligible: false` and a bounded `unsupportedReason`, no\n114\tcomponent rows are invented for it, and quoting it is a stable\n115\t`409 OFFER_NOT_QUOTE_ELIGIBLE`.\n116\t\n117\t## Records (all read-only publicly)\n118\t\n119\t`product`, `product-version`, `price-book`, `offer`, `price-component`,\n120\t`price-tier`, `catalog-sync-run`, `quote`, `quote-line`, `quote-version`,\n121\t`quote-version-line`, `quote-version-component`, `quote-version-total`,\n122\t`quote-approval` — capabilities `get`/`list` only. No\n123\tpublic create or update exists on any of them: records are produced solely by\n124\tcatalog sync and the quote actions through the trusted in-process\n125\t`createManaged`/`applyManaged` path, so no client can forge a price, a total or\n126\ta version. Correctness reads use exact `listWhere`/`countWhere` over\n127\tmanifest-declared indexes, never a paged scan.\n128\t\n129\t## Money and discounts\n130\t\n131\tAmounts are **safe integers in 1/100 currency units**, two decimals — the\n132\tADR-014 contract, deliberately not ISO-4217 exponents. Currencies are uppercase\n133\t`[A-Z]{3}`; a quote binds one price book and one currency and there is **no\n134\tconversion**. Discounts are **integer basis points** 0–10000 (`1000` = 10.00%).\n135\t\n136\tRounding, exactly:\n137\t\n138\t```text\n139\tcomponentList     = per pricing model (integer arithmetic, overflow-checked)\n140\tcomponentDiscount = trunc(componentList × bps ÷ 10000)   (never rounds up)\n141\tcomponentNet      = componentList − componentDiscount    (authoritative)\n142\tline/group totals = checked sums of component amounts\n143\t```\n144\t\n145\tThe line's basis-point discount applies **uniformly to every component of that\n146\tline, after tier/list calculation**; each component keeps its own list,\n147\tdiscount and net amounts. Any overflow is a refusal, never a silently wrong\n148\tnumber.\n149\t\n150\t### Totals are grouped — there is no grand total\n151\t\n152\tA quote and every quote version persist **one one-time total plus one total per\n153\t`(currency, interval, intervalCount)`**:\n154\t\n155\t```json\n156\t{\n157\t  \"oneTimeTotal\": { \"currency\": \"EUR\", \"netAmountCents\": 500000 },\n158\t  \"recurringTotals\": [\n159\t    { \"currency\": \"EUR\", \"interval\": \"month\", \"intervalCount\": 1, \"netAmountCents\": 280000 },\n160\t    { \"currency\": \"EUR\", \"interval\": \"year\",  \"intervalCount\": 1, \"netAmountCents\": 1000000 }\n161\t  ]\n162\t}\n163\t```\n164\t\n165\tUnlike periods are never summed. **ARR, MRR and TCV are deliberately not\n166\tderived** — contract term and normalization policy are not modeled.\n167\t\n168\t## Catalog identity and immutability\n169\t\n170\tA **Product** is stable identity; a **ProductVersion** is an immutable\n171\tcommercial description; an **Offer is versioned as a whole** — its name,\n172\teligibility, every component and every tier are fingerprinted together, so any\n173\tpricing change creates a **new immutable offer revision** with fresh component\n174\tand tier rows while the prior revision is deactivated. Unchanged data writes\n175\tnothing (no fake audits/events). Quoted evidence is never rewritten by later\n176\tcatalog movement, and a superseded revision cannot be added to a new draft\n177\t(`409 OFFER_INACTIVE`). A draft line always re-prices from its **pinned** offer\n178\trevision. Provider failures are stable\n179\t`PROVIDER_FAILED` / `PROVIDER_TIMEOUT` / `PROVIDER_INVALID` with **no partial\n180\tcatalog state** and an honest failed trace. Provider-managed products missing\n181\tfrom a payload deactivate only under the provider's declared\n182\t`config.deactivateMissing`.\n183\t\n184\tFuture provider packages may target Stripe Products/Prices, Zuora, ERP or\n185\tcustom CPQ APIs. **No real adapter or credential ships in this milestone** —\n186\tthe starter's deterministic fixture provider proves the contract offline.\n187\t\n188\t## Discount policy and approval\n189\t\n190\tPolicies are code-first, versioned definitions with declared JSON-safe\n191\t`config` (thresholds live there, never in closures — the config is part of the\n192\tdeclared-definition fingerprint, so changing a threshold without publishing a\n193\tnew version stops the boot). `evaluate` gets a deep-frozen context and returns\n194\t**synchronously** `auto_approve | approval_required | reject` with bounded\n195\treason/approval-key/matched-rule metadata. The decision, policy name, version\n196\tand fingerprint are stored on the Quote Version, so historical quotes stay\n197\texplainable after a v2 ships.\n198\t\n199\t`auto_approve` approves with **no approval record**; `approval_required`\n200\tcreates exactly one approval per version and parks the quote in\n201\t`pending_approval`; `reject` marks it rejected with the policy reason.\n202\t\n203\t**The human boundary:** only `actor.type === 'user'` may approve or reject — an\n204\tagent actor is refused `403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`. `requiredApprovalKey`\n205\t(e.g. `sales-manager`) is a **label**, not enforced security: real\n206\tSales-Manager/Finance role enforcement requires the Production Spine\n207\t(authentication, tenancy, RBAC — `docs/strategy/EXECUTION_ROADMAP.md` Phase 6).\n208\t\n209\t## Lifecycle\n210\t\n211\t```text\n212\tdraft ──add/update/remove line──▶ draft ──submit──▶ pending_approval ──approve──▶ approved (terminal in M10)\n213\t                                                  └─reject──▶ rejected ──revise──▶ draft ──submit──▶ version 2\n214\t```\n215\t\n216\tEvery rule is server-enforced (`fromStates`), not UI-enforced. Draft edits are\n217\toptimistic-concurrency guarded by `expectedRevision` (`409 STALE_REVISION`);\n218\tversion numbers are DB-monotonic so concurrent submits produce exactly one\n219\tversion; one decision per version (`409 ALREADY_DECIDED`).\n220\t\n221\t## What a Quote Version snapshots\n222\t\n223\tEverything needed to reproduce the commercial decision after any catalog\n224\tchange: offer identity + revision, product version, every component definition\n225\t(charge type, pricing model, recurrence, amounts), the complete tier schedule,\n226\tthe calculated tier breakdown, quantity, list/discount/net per component,\n227\tprovider provenance, and one grouped-total row per period. Proven: a provider\n228\ttier-boundary and price change creates new offer revisions while every existing\n229\tQuote Version stays byte-identical, and new drafts price at the new revision.\n230\t\n231\t## Evidence\n232\t\n233\t`tests/commercial-contract.test.js`, `tests/commercial-e2e.test.js`,\n234\t`tests/admin-quotes.test.js`,\n235\t`examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/install.mjs`,\n236\t`docs/plans/milestone-10-commercial-operations.md`.   // requires actor.type === 'user'\n```\n\nThe Admin adds a **Quotes** view (`#/quotes`, `#/quotes/<id>`) for browsing a\nprice book, editing draft lines, submitting and deciding — every amount is\nserver-calculated and every mutation is the same server action.\n\n## The pricing model\n\n```text\nProduct → ProductVersion → Offer (rate plan) → PriceComponent(s) → Tier(s)\n```\n\nAn **Offer** is the sellable package; it may carry several **PriceComponents**\nmixing one-time and recurring charges. Each component declares:\n\n```text\nchargeType     one_time | recurring\npricingModel   flat_fee | per_unit | volume | graduated\ninterval       month | year        (null for one_time)\nintervalCount  positive integer    (null for one_time)\ntiers          ordered schedule    (volume/graduated only)\nprovenance     provider, providerVersion, externalProductId,\n               externalOfferId, externalPriceId, sourcePricingModel,\n               sourceFingerprint\n```\n\nA tier carries `upTo` (inclusive upper bound; `null` on the single final\nopen-ended tier), `unitAmountCents` and an explicit optional `flatAmountCents`.\nSchedules must be strictly increasing and end open-ended, so they are gap-free\nand overlap-free by construction.\n\nExample composite offer — **one quote line, one quantity**:\n\n```text\nEnterprise Plan\n  1. Setup and migration   one_time  + flat_fee   EUR 5,000.00\n  2. Platform fee          recurring + flat_fee   EUR 2,000.00 / month\n  3. Seats                 recurring + volume     1–20: EUR 50.00, 21–100: EUR 40.00, 101+: EUR 30.00 per seat/month\n```\n\n### Quantity semantics (explicit, never silent)\n\n| Model | Line quantity behavior |\n|---|---|\n| `flat_fee` | charged **once** per line — quantity never multiplies it |\n| `per_unit` | `unitAmount × quantity` |\n| `volume` | the tier the **total** quantity reaches prices the **entire** quantity (+ that tier's flat amount once) |\n| `graduated` | each band prices the quantity inside it (+ each receiving band's flat amount once) |\n\nAt 30 seats the example above bills setup once (not 30×), the platform fee\nonce per month, and 30 × EUR 40.00 for seats (the whole quantity at the\nreached tier).\n\n### Provider mapping\n\n| Provider model | Normalized |\n|---|---|\n| Stripe one-time price | `one_time` |\n| Stripe recurring price | `recurring` + interval/intervalCount |\n| Stripe `tiers_mode: volume` | `volume` |\n| Stripe `tiers_mode: graduated` | `graduated` |\n| Zuora one-time charge | `one_time` |\n| Zuora recurring charge | `recurring` |\n| Zuora Volume Pricing | `volume` |\n| Zuora Tiered/Cumulative Pricing | `graduated` |\n| Stripe metered / Zuora usage | **unsupported** → `quoteEligible: false` |\n\n`sourcePricingModel` and the external ids keep provider provenance intact.\n**No real Stripe/Zuora/ERP adapter or credential ships** — a deterministic\nfixture provider proves the contract offline, and full Stripe or Zuora support\nis not claimed.\n\n### Unsupported models fail closed\n\nMetered usage, overage, proration, ramp deals, minimum commitments,\nattribute-based/dynamic pricing, tax-inclusive computation, FX and custom\nprovider formulas are **never approximated as flat prices**. The offer is\nstored with `quoteEligible: false` and a bounded `unsupportedReason`, no\ncomponent rows are invented for it, and quoting it is a stable\n`409 OFFER_NOT_QUOTE_ELIGIBLE`.\n\n## Records (all read-only publicly)\n\n`product`, `product-version`, `price-book`, `offer`, `price-component`,\n`price-tier`, `catalog-sync-run`, `quote`, `quote-line`, `quote-version`,\n`quote-version-line`, `quote-version-component`, `quote-version-total`,\n`quote-approval` — capabilities `get`/`list` only. No\npublic create or update exists on any of them: records are produced solely by\ncatalog sync and the quote actions through the trusted in-process\n`createManaged`/`applyManaged` path, so no client can forge a price, a total or\na version. Correctness reads use exact `listWhere`/`countWhere` over\nmanifest-declared indexes, never a paged scan.\n\n## Money and discounts\n\nAmounts are **safe integers in 1/100 currency units**, two decimals — the\nADR-014 contract, deliberately not ISO-4217 exponents. Currencies are uppercase\n`[A-Z]{3}`; a quote binds one price book and one currency and there is **no\nconversion**. Discounts are **integer basis points** 0–10000 (`1000` = 10.00%).\n\nRounding, exactly:\n\n```text\ncomponentList     = per pricing model (integer arithmetic, overflow-checked)\ncomponentDiscount = trunc(componentList × bps ÷ 10000)   (never rounds up)\ncomponentNet      = componentList − componentDiscount    (authoritative)\nline/group totals = checked sums of component amounts\n```\n\nThe line's basis-point discount applies **uniformly to every component of that\nline, after tier/list calculation**; each component keeps its own list,\ndiscount and net amounts. Any overflow is a refusal, never a silently wrong\nnumber.\n\n### Totals are grouped — there is no grand total\n\nA quote and every quote version persist **one one-time total plus one total per\n`(currency, interval, intervalCount)`**:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"oneTimeTotal\": { \"currency\": \"EUR\", \"netAmountCents\": 500000 },\n  \"recurringTotals\": [\n    { \"currency\": \"EUR\", \"interval\": \"month\", \"intervalCount\": 1, \"netAmountCents\": 280000 },\n    { \"currency\": \"EUR\", \"interval\": \"year\",  \"intervalCount\": 1, \"netAmountCents\": 1000000 }\n  ]\n}\n```\n\nUnlike periods are never summed. **ARR, MRR and TCV are deliberately not\nderived** — contract term and normalization policy are not modeled.\n\n## Catalog identity and immutability\n\nA **Product** is stable identity; a **ProductVersion** is an immutable\ncommercial description; an **Offer is versioned as a whole** — its name,\neligibility, every component and every tier are fingerprinted together, so any\npricing change creates a **new immutable offer revision** with fresh component\nand tier rows while the prior revision is deactivated. Unchanged data writes\nnothing (no fake audits/events). Quoted evidence is never rewritten by later\ncatalog movement, and a superseded revision cannot be added to a new draft\n(`409 OFFER_INACTIVE`). A draft line always re-prices from its **pinned** offer\nrevision. Provider failures are stable\n`PROVIDER_FAILED` / `PROVIDER_TIMEOUT` / `PROVIDER_INVALID` with **no partial\ncatalog state** and an honest failed trace. Provider-managed products missing\nfrom a payload deactivate only under the provider's declared\n`config.deactivateMissing`.\n\nFuture provider packages may target Stripe Products/Prices, Zuora, ERP or\ncustom CPQ APIs. **No real adapter or credential ships in this milestone** —\nthe starter's deterministic fixture provider proves the contract offline.\n\n## Discount policy and approval\n\nPolicies are code-first, versioned definitions with declared JSON-safe\n`config` (thresholds live there, never in closures — the config is part of the\ndeclared-definition fingerprint, so changing a threshold without publishing a\nnew version stops the boot). `evaluate` gets a deep-frozen context and returns\n**synchronously** `auto_approve | approval_required | reject` with bounded\nreason/approval-key/matched-rule metadata. The decision, policy name, version\nand fingerprint are stored on the Quote Version, so historical quotes stay\nexplainable after a v2 ships.\n\n`auto_approve` approves with **no approval record**; `approval_required`\ncreates exactly one approval per version and parks the quote in\n`pending_approval`; `reject` marks it rejected with the policy reason.\n\n**The human boundary:** only `actor.type === 'user'` may approve or reject — an\nagent actor is refused `403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`. `requiredApprovalKey`\n(e.g. `sales-manager`) is a **label**, not enforced security: real\nSales-Manager/Finance role enforcement requires the Production Spine\n(authentication, tenancy, RBAC — `docs/strategy/EXECUTION_ROADMAP.md` Phase 6).\n\n## Lifecycle\n\n```text\ndraft ──add/update/remove line──▶ draft ──submit──▶ pending_approval ──approve──▶ approved (terminal in M10)\n                                                  └─reject──▶ rejected ──revise──▶ draft ──submit──▶ version 2\n```\n\nEvery rule is server-enforced (`fromStates`), not UI-enforced. Draft edits are\noptimistic-concurrency guarded by `expectedRevision` (`409 STALE_REVISION`);\nversion numbers are DB-monotonic so concurrent submits produce exactly one\nversion; one decision per version (`409 ALREADY_DECIDED`).\n\n## What a Quote Version snapshots\n\nEverything needed to reproduce the commercial decision after any catalog\nchange: offer identity + revision, product version, every component definition\n(charge type, pricing model, recurrence, amounts), the complete tier schedule,\nthe calculated tier breakdown, quantity, list/discount/net per component,\nprovider provenance, and one grouped-total row per period. Proven: a provider\ntier-boundary and price change creates new offer revisions while every existing\nQuote Version stays byte-identical, and new drafts price at the new revision.\n\n## Evidence\n\n`tests/commercial-contract.test.js`, `tests/commercial-e2e.test.js`,\n`tests/admin-quotes.test.js`,\n`examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/install.mjs`,\n`docs/plans/milestone-10-commercial-operations.md`. Agent instructions:\n`.claude/skills/build-commercial-operations/SKILL.md` (this file is the\nCodex-readable mirror).\n","numLines":239,"startLine":1,"totalLines":239}}}

AND version = ?',\n      );\n      const insert = database.raw.prepare(\n        'INSERT INTO definition_versions(id, type, name, version, fingerprint, registered_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)',\n      );\n      for (const { type, entry } of entries) {\n        const { name, version } = entry.definition;\n        const existing = select.get(type, name, version);\n        if (existing === undefined) {\n          insert.run(crypto.randomUUID(), type, name, version, entry.fingerprint, new Date().toISOString());\n          continue;\n        }\n        if (String(existing.fingerprint) !== entry.fingerprint) {\n          throw new ValidationError(\n            `${type} \"${name}@${version}\" source changed after registration (persisted fingerprint ${String(existing.fingerprint).slice(0, 12)}…, current ${entry.fingerprint.slice(0, 12)}…). ` +\n              'Registered definition versions are immutable: publish a new version instead of editing this one.',\n          );\n        }\n      }\n    });\n  }\n\n  /** Serializable, function-free metadata for /api/schema. */\n  metadata() {\n    return {\n      commercialContract: 1,\n      pricingContract: 1,\n      money: 'safe integers, 1/100 currency units, two decimals (not ISO-4217 exponents)',\n      discounts: 'integer basis points 0-10000; discount = trunc(list*bps/10000), applied per component after tier calculation',\n      chargeTypes: [...CHARGE_TYPES],\n      pricingModels: [...PRICING_MODELS],\n      recurringIntervals: [...RECURRING_INTERVALS],\n      maxDiscountBps: MAX_DISCOUNT_BPS,\n      maxQuantity: MAX_QUANTITY,\n      quantitySemantics: {\n        flat_fee: 'charged once per quote line; line quantity does not multiply it',\n        per_unit: 'unitAmount x quantity',\n        volume: 'the tier the total quantity reaches prices the entire quantity',\n        graduated: 'each tier band prices the quantity that falls inside it',\n      },\n      totals: 'grouped: one one-time total plus one per (currency, interval, intervalCount); unlike periods are never summed and no ARR/MRR/TCV is derived',\n      unsupportedModels: 'metered usage, overage, proration, ramps, minimum commitments, attribute-based pricing, taxes and FX are never approximated: such offers persist with quoteEligible=false and an unsupportedReason',\n      catalogProviders: [...this.catalogProviders.values()]\n        .sort((a, b) => (a.definition.name < b.definition.name ? -1 : 1))\n        .map(({ definition, fingerprint }) => ({\n          name: definition.name,\n          version: definition.version,\n          label: definition.label ?? definition.name,\n          fingerprint,\n        })),\n      discountPolicies: [...this.discountPolicies.values()]\n        .sort((a, b) => (a.definition.name === b.definition.name\n          ? a.definition.version - b.definition.version\n          : a.definition.name < b.definition.name ? -1 : 1))\n        .map(({ definition, fingerprint }) => ({\n          name: definition.name,\n          version: definition.version,\n          label: definition.label ?? definition.name,\n          fingerprint,\n        })),\n    };\n  }\n}\n\nexport { requireCurrency };\n","numLines":236,"startLine":1,"totalLines":236}}}

{"type":"system","subtype":"task_summary","detail":"Running sed -n '186,210p' /home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs…","uuid":"9a2df97a-3636-4c55-84fc-abb74272400f","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6"}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_017TBnptaFX6KsuNbUSV9hU1","type":"tool_result","content":"## ADR-016 — Commercial Operations runs on bounded primitives, immutable versions and versioned discount policy\n\n**Status:** accepted\n\nCommercial Operations v1 (Milestone 10) — catalog, quotes, discount approval — compared three shapes: **generic generated modules plus actions only** (prices and totals would be client-writable and quoted evidence mutable — the anti-bypass and immutability requirements are structurally unreachable), a **monolithic handwritten CPQ core** (maximum control, but it duplicates the validation, savepoints, audit, events, read-only capabilities, exact queries and indexes the factory already generates, and forks the storage conventions the rest of the framework proves), and **bounded commercial primitives on the hardened M9 machinery (chosen)**. Storage is ten starter-generated **read-only all-managed modules** (ADR-015 pattern: capabilities `['get','list']`, no public create/update at all, trusted `createManaged`/`applyManaged`, exact `listWhere`/`countWhere` over manifest-declared indexes). Behavior is framework-owned code-first source: `commercial-registry.js` (catalog providers + versioned discount policies on the ADR-015 declared-definition fingerprint mechanism), `catalog-sync.js`, `commercial-money.js` and `commercial-actions.js`. **No pricing-expression language, no CPQ/rules DSL.** Company and Opportunity are referenced, never duplicated; `create-quote` is an action on the action-eligible core Opportunity module (ADR-014 precedent). Signature and Order are Milestone 11 and are deliberately absent — the immutable Quote Version is exactly the artifact they will consume.\n\n**Catalog identity is stable; commercial evidence is immutable.** A **Product** carries stable identity (source kind internal/provider, provider, external id, DB-unique source key, active, current version link); a **ProductVersion** is an immutable commercial description; a **PriceBook** is a named list in exactly one immutable currency; an **Offer (rate plan)** is the sellable package bound to one ProductVersion, carrying one or more **PriceComponents** with their **Tier** schedules. Changing commercial data never rewrites history: sync computes a declared **source fingerprint** per product and per **whole offer** (name, eligibility, every component and every tier), and a change creates a **new version / new offer revision** with fresh immutable component and tier rows, deactivating the prior revision — a quote's frozen evidence is untouched by later catalog movement, and re-adding a superseded revision is refused (`409 OFFER_INACTIVE`). `syncCatalog` follows the ADR-015 external-call discipline: `fetchCatalog` runs **outside** any transaction under a bounded timeout with late settlement abandoned (never cancelled — no AbortSignal claim), the payload is validated into the contract (bounded strings, safe integers, canonical currencies, unique source keys; anything else is a stable `PROVIDER_INVALID`), and reconciliation runs in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction with a `CatalogSyncRun` recording provider identity, fingerprint and counts. Identical input re-syncs are **idempotent** — unchanged identities produce no writes, audits or events. Provider-managed products absent from a payload are deactivated **only** under the provider's declared `config.deactivateMissing`. No provider secrets or raw payloads are stored. Future provider packages may target Stripe Products/Prices, Zuora, ERP or custom CPQ APIs; **no real adapter or credential ships in M10** — a deterministic fixture provider proves the contract.\n\n**The pricing model is composite, not one flat price per product (corrected in the adversarial review).** The original model — one sellable `PriceBookEntry` carrying a single unit price — could not express a real offer, and would have forced provider catalogs to be flattened. The corrected shape is `Product → ProductVersion → Offer → PriceComponent(s) → Tier(s)`. A component declares `chargeType` (`one_time` | `recurring`), `pricingModel` (`flat_fee` | `per_unit` | `volume` | `graduated`), `interval`/`intervalCount` for recurring charges, amounts, an ordered tier schedule for tiered models, and provider provenance (provider, version, external product/offer/price ids, `sourcePricingModel`, source fingerprint). One Offer can therefore mix a one-time setup fee, a recurring monthly platform fee and recurring volume-tiered seats, and **one quote line selects one Offer and a quantity**.\n\n**Quantity semantics are explicit, never silent:** `flat_fee` is charged ONCE per line (line quantity never multiplies it), `per_unit` multiplies by quantity, `volume` prices the entire quantity at the single tier the quantity reaches (plus that tier's flat amount once), and `graduated` prices each band independently (plus each receiving band's flat amount once). Tier schedules are validated fail-closed: non-empty for tiered models, forbidden for non-tiered ones, strictly increasing inclusive `upTo` bounds with implicit lower bounds (so the schedule is gap-free and overlap-free by construction), exactly one open-ended final tier, non-negative safe-integer amounts, at most 50 tiers, quantities within the supported range. Every calculation returns a deterministic **tier breakdown** that sums to the component total.\n\n**Provider semantics are preserved, and unsupported models fail closed.** The normalized model maps Stripe one_time/recurring and `tiers_mode` volume/graduated, and Zuora one-time/recurring charges with Volume and Tiered/Cumulative pricing, onto the same four models while keeping the provider's own model name and external ids. Models the framework does not implement — metered usage, overage, proration, ramps, minimum commitments, attribute-based/dynamic pricing, tax-inclusive computation, FX, custom formulas — are **never approximated as flat prices**: the offer persists with `quoteEligible: false` and a bounded `unsupportedReason`, no component rows are invented for it, and quoting it is a stable `409 OFFER_NOT_QUOTE_ELIGIBLE`. No real Stripe/Zuora adapter or credential ships; a deterministic fixture provider proves the contract offline, and full Stripe/Zuora support is explicitly not claimed.\n\n**Totals are grouped by commercial period; there is no grand total.** A quote and every quote version persist one **one-time** total plus one total per `(currency, interval, intervalCount)`. Unlike periods are never summed, and **ARR/MRR/TCV are deliberately not derived** because contract term and normalization policy are not modeled. Discount policy (v1, documented): a line's basis-point discount applies **uniformly to every component of that line, after tier/list calculation**; each component retains list, discount and net amounts, and the policy evaluates the complete grouped totals plus the component mix.\n\n**A submitted Quote Version snapshots everything needed to reproduce the commercial decision**: the offer identity and revision, product version, every component definition (charge type, pricing model, recurrence, amounts), the complete tier schedule, the calculated tier breakdown, quantity, list/discount/net per component, provider provenance, and one grouped-total row per period. A later catalog sync — including tier-boundary and price changes — leaves every existing Quote Version byte-identical, while new drafts price from the new active revision (both proven).\n\n**Money and discounts are safe-integer arithmetic with a documented rounding policy.** Amounts remain ADR-014's contract: safe integers in 1/100 currency units, two decimals, deliberately **not** ISO-4217 exponents. Currencies are uppercase `[A-Z]{3}`; a Quote binds one Price Book and one currency, and there is **no conversion** — a mismatched entry is `409 CURRENCY_MISMATCH`. Discounts are integer **basis points** 0–10000 (no floating-point percentages). Rounding, exactly: each component's list amount is computed per its pricing model with integer arithmetic only; `componentDiscount = trunc(componentList × bps ÷ 10000)` (truncating, so a discount never rounds up); `componentNet = componentList − componentDiscount` is **authoritative**; line and grouped totals are checked sums of component amounts. Every multiplication and sum is overflow-checked — a result outside the safe range is a refusal, never a silently wrong number. **The server derives every price and total from catalog data; a client cannot submit a unit price or a total through any surface.**\n\n**Quote lifecycle is server-authoritative and version history is immutable.** `draft → pending_approval → approved | rejected`, with `revise` reopening a rejected quote and `approved` terminal in M10. Draft edits (`add-line`/`update-line`/`remove-line`, soft removal — the framework has no safe general delete) are gated to `draft` by `fromStates`, validate that the offer revision is active, quote-eligible, complete, belongs to the quote's Price Book and matches its currency (a line re-prices only from its **pinned** offer revision, so a later catalog revision never silently re-prices an existing draft), and each successful edit recalculates totals and increments `draftRevision`; an optional `expectedRevision` gives optimistic concurrency (`409 STALE_REVISION`), so concurrent editors cannot lose updates. `submit` freezes an immutable **QuoteVersion** plus **QuoteVersionLines** (product, version, SKU, entry id + revision, quantity, list/net unit, discount bps, subtotal/discount/total, pricing mode/interval) whose number is DB-monotonic through a unique `qv:<quote>:<n>` key — concurrent submits produce exactly one version. Fault injection at any step rolls the whole submission back: no partial version, no orphan approval, the quote unchanged.\n\n**Discount policy is versioned, explainable and deterministic; approval is human.** A code-first `defineDiscountPolicy`-shaped definition carries `{name, version, label, config, evaluate}` on the ADR-015 mechanism: declared JSON-safe config is fingerprinted (thresholds live there, never in closures), drift under a registered version stops the boot, rollback is a new version. `evaluate` receives a deep-frozen context (quote identity, totals, max-line and effective discount bps, line summaries, opportunity evidence where readable, actor, frozen config) and must return **synchronously** one of `auto_approve | approval_required | reject` with bounded reason/approval-key/matched-rule metadata; Promises and out-of-contract results are refused. The decision, policy name, version and fingerprint are stored **on the Quote Version**, so a historical quote stays explainable after policy v2 ships. `auto_approve` approves the quote with **no fake approval record**; `approval_required` creates exactly one `quote-approval` (DB-unique per version) and parks the quote in `pending_approval`; `reject` marks the quote rejected with the policy's reason. `approve`/`reject` actions enforce the framework's human boundary — **only `actor.type === 'user'` may decide; an agent actor is refused `403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`** — with one decision per version (a second is `409 ALREADY_DECIDED`), the decision and the quote lifecycle committing atomically, and concurrent decisions resolving to one winner. The core Approval module is renewal/opportunity-shaped (its table requires an opportunity FK), so quotes use a dedicated approval record rather than bending that schema; generalizing the approval domain is future work. **`requiredApprovalKey` is a label, not enforced security: real Sales-Manager/Finance role enforcement requires the Production Spine (authentication, tenancy, RBAC), and nothing here claims otherwise.**\n\n## ADR-017 — External signature runs on a bounded provider contract, a persisted state machine and explicit reconciliation","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"502b948e-522b-4921-8cf1-0e03212751ad","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:16:10.111Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"## ADR-016 — Commercial Operations runs on bounded primitives, immutable versions and versioned discount policy\n\n**Status:** accepted\n\nCommercial Operations v1 (Milestone 10) — catalog, quotes, discount approval — compared three shapes: **generic generated modules plus actions only** (prices and totals would be client-writable and quoted evidence mutable — the anti-bypass and immutability requirements are structurally unreachable), a **monolithic handwritten CPQ core** (maximum control, but it duplicates the validation, savepoints, audit, events, read-only capabilities, exact queries and indexes the factory already generates, and forks the storage conventions the rest of the framework proves), and **bounded commercial primitives on the hardened M9 machinery (chosen)**. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_019fN5ZPidVxHuZrhAeMxZ6Q","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tname: build-commercial-operations\n3\tdescription: Add or extend Commercial Operations in an Accordo project - catalog providers, products and price books, quotes with server-priced lines, immutable quote versions, and versioned discount policies with human approval. Use for catalog/quote/discount/CPQ work. Do not use for CRUD module changes (create-crm-module) or lead scoring/routing (build-lead-intelligence).\n4\trequires:\n5\t  tier: generated-project\n6\t  command: \"crm app inspect\"\n7\t  projectSurface: [\"packages/commercial/generated/index.js\", \"packages/core/src/commercial-money.js\"]\n8\t  repositorySurface: [\"ARCHITECTURE.md\", \"DECISIONS.md\", \"docs/COMMERCIAL_OPERATIONS.md\"]\n9\t  degradesTo: \"the composed commercial packages, actions, policies and providers reported by `crm app inspect --json`\"\n10\t---\n11\t\n12\t## Orient yourself first\n13\t\n14\t```bash\n15\tnpm run crm -- app inspect --json\n16\t```\n17\t\n18\tRead `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]`, in that order. Every problem is fixed or reported before anything is built on top of it, and **every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim.** Then read `packages[]`, `capabilities[]`, `resources[]`, `actions[]`, `policies[]` and `providers[]`: that list is what exists. A capability absent from the report does not exist, whatever a record name, a label or a document suggests.\n19\t\n20\tIf the repository documents this skill names are absent, you are in a project built from this framework rather than in the framework itself. The inspection report is then the source of truth and those documents are optional background — do not guess at their contents, and do not assume a path exists because this skill names it.\n21\t\n22\t**Background, where they exist:** `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md` (ADR-016) and `docs/COMMERCIAL_OPERATIONS.md`. They are the deeper source for the rules below, not a prerequisite for them — the rules stand on their own.\n23\t\n24\t## Integrate a catalog provider\n25\t\n26\t1. Define it code-first: `{ name, version, label, config, async fetchCatalog(input, ctx) }` returning `{ sourceRef?, priceBooks[], products[], offers[] }`. An **offer** is the sellable package: `{ sourceKey, priceBookSourceKey, productSourceKey, name, active, externalOfferId?, components[] }`. A **component** is `{ sourceKey, label, chargeType: 'one_time'|'recurring', pricingModel: 'flat_fee'|'per_unit'|'volume'|'graduated', interval?, intervalCount?, unitAmountCents? (per_unit), flatAmountCents? (flat_fee), tiers? (volume/graduated), externalPriceId?, sourcePricingModel? }`. Tiers are ordered `{ upTo (inclusive, null on the final open-ended tier), unitAmountCents, flatAmountCents? }`. Out-of-contract output is refused (`PROVIDER_INVALID`) — never loosen the normalizer.\n27\t2. **Never flatten a provider model.** Map Stripe one_time/recurring and `tiers_mode` volume/graduated, and Zuora one-time/recurring with Volume and Tiered/Cumulative pricing, onto the four supported models and keep `sourcePricingModel` + external ids. For anything unsupported (metered usage, overage, proration, ramps, minimums, attribute-based pricing), mark the component `unsupportedModel: '<name>'` — the offer persists `quoteEligible: false` with a reason and is refused for quoting. Approximating it as a flat price is a defect.\n28\t3. Register it in `packages/commercial/generated/index.js` (static import, like actions/pipelines/intelligence).\n29\t4. The provider is called **outside** the write transaction under a bounded timeout. Never add DB writes to a provider.\n30\t5. Sync is idempotent by source key + declared source fingerprint: unchanged data must produce no writes/audits/events; changed data must create a **new** product version or **whole-offer revision** (with fresh component and tier rows), never an in-place edit. Historical and quoted evidence is immutable.\n31\t6. Test failure paths with a deterministic fixture (outage → `PROVIDER_FAILED`, hang → `PROVIDER_TIMEOUT`, bad shapes → `PROVIDER_INVALID`): no partial catalog, honest failed trace.\n32\t7. Real Stripe/Zuora/ERP adapters need human-approved credentials and are out of scope until then; do not claim full Stripe or Zuora support.\n33\t\n34\t## Build a price book and quote flow\n35\t\n36\t1. Storage modules are **read-only publicly** (every field `writable: \"managed\"` → capabilities `get`/`list`, no public create/update). Never add a public write path to a commercial record; use the trusted `createManaged`/`applyManaged` from action code.\n37\t2. Every price and total is **server-derived** from catalog data. A client may send `offerId`, `quantity` and `discountBps` — never an amount, a tier or a total.\n38\t3. Use `computeComponentAmount`/`computeLineBreakdown`/`groupComponentTotals` from `packages/core/src/commercial-money.js`. Do not hand-roll money arithmetic and never use floating-point percentages. Respect quantity semantics: **flat fees are charged once per line**, per-unit multiplies, volume prices the whole quantity at the reached tier, graduated prices each band.\n39\t4. **Never combine unlike periods.** Persist and display the one-time total plus one total per `(currency, interval, intervalCount)`. Do not derive ARR/MRR/TCV — contract term is not modeled.\n40\t5. Guard draft edits with `expectedRevision` (optimistic concurrency) and gate every action with `fromStates`. A quote binds one price book and one currency; a mismatched, inactive, incomplete or quote-ineligible offer is refused. A draft line re-prices only from its pinned offer revision.\n41\t6. Never delete records: soft-remove draft lines.\n42\t\n43\t## Create a discount policy version\n44\t\n45\t1. `{ name, version, label, config, evaluate(context) }` — `evaluate` gets a deep-frozen context (totals, `maxLineDiscountBps`, `effectiveDiscountBps`, lines, opportunity evidence, actor, frozen `config`) and must return **synchronously** `{ decision: 'auto_approve'|'approval_required'|'reject', reason?, requiredApprovalKey?, matchedRule? }`. Use for catalog/quote/discount/CPQ work. Do not use for CRUD module changes (create-crm-module) or lead scoring/routing (build-lead-intelligence).\nrequires:\n  tier: generated-project\n  command: \"crm app inspect\"\n  projectSurface: [\"packages/commercial/generated/index.js\", \"packages/core/src/commercial-money.js\"]\n  repositorySurface: [\"ARCHITECTURE.md\", \"DECISIONS.md\", \"docs/COMMERCIAL_OPERATIONS.md\"]\n  degradesTo: \"the composed commercial packages, actions, policies and providers reported by `crm app inspect --json`\"\n---\n\n## Orient yourself first\n\n```bash\nnpm run crm -- app inspect --json\n```\n\nRead `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]`, in that order. Every problem is fixed or reported before anything is built on top of it, and **every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim.** Then read `packages[]`, `capabilities[]`, `resources[]`, `actions[]`, `policies[]` and `providers[]`: that list is what exists. A capability absent from the report does not exist, whatever a record name, a label or a document suggests.\n\nIf the repository documents this skill names are absent, you are in a project built from this framework rather than in the framework itself. The inspection report is then the source of truth and those documents are optional background — do not guess at their contents, and do not assume a path exists because this skill names it.\n\n**Background, where they exist:** `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md` (ADR-016) and `docs/COMMERCIAL_OPERATIONS.md`. They are the deeper source for the rules below, not a prerequisite for them — the rules stand on their own.\n\n## Integrate a catalog provider\n\n1. Define it code-first: `{ name, version, label, config, async fetchCatalog(input, ctx) }` returning `{ sourceRef?, priceBooks[], products[], offers[] }`. An **offer** is the sellable package: `{ sourceKey, priceBookSourceKey, productSourceKey, name, active, externalOfferId?, components[] }`. A **component** is `{ sourceKey, label, chargeType: 'one_time'|'recurring', pricingModel: 'flat_fee'|'per_unit'|'volume'|'graduated', interval?, intervalCount?, unitAmountCents? (per_unit), flatAmountCents? (flat_fee), tiers? (volume/graduated), externalPriceId?, sourcePricingModel? }`. Tiers are ordered `{ upTo (inclusive, null on the final open-ended tier), unitAmountCents, flatAmountCents? }`. Out-of-contract output is refused (`PROVIDER_INVALID`) — never loosen the normalizer.\n2. **Never flatten a provider model.** Map Stripe one_time/recurring and `tiers_mode` volume/graduated, and Zuora one-time/recurring with Volume and Tiered/Cumulative pricing, onto the four supported models and keep `sourcePricingModel` + external ids. For anything unsupported (metered usage, overage, proration, ramps, minimums, attribute-based pricing), mark the component `unsupportedModel: '<name>'` — the offer persists `quoteEligible: false` with a reason and is refused for quoting. Approximating it as a flat price is a defect.\n3. Register it in `packages/commercial/generated/index.js` (static import, like actions/pipelines/intelligence).\n4. The provider is called **outside** the write transaction under a bounded timeout. Never add DB writes to a provider.\n5. Sync is idempotent by source key + declared source fingerprint: unchanged data must produce no writes/audits/events; changed data must create a **new** product version or **whole-offer revision** (with fresh component and tier rows), never an in-place edit. Historical and quoted evidence is immutable.\n6. Test failure paths with a deterministic fixture (outage → `PROVIDER_FAILED`, hang → `PROVIDER_TIMEOUT`, bad shapes → `PROVIDER_INVALID`): no partial catalog, honest failed trace.\n7. Real Stripe/Zuora/ERP adapters need human-approved credentials and are out of scope until then; do not claim full Stripe or Zuora support.\n\n## Build a price book and quote flow\n\n1. Storage modules are **read-only publicly** (every field `writable: \"managed\"` → capabilities `get`/`list`, no public create/update). Never add a public write path to a commercial record; use the trusted `createManaged`/`applyManaged` from action code.\n2. Every price and total is **server-derived** from catalog data. A client may send `offerId`, `quantity` and `discountBps` — never an amount, a tier or a total.\n3. Use `computeComponentAmount`/`computeLineBreakdown`/`groupComponentTotals` from `packages/core/src/commercial-money.js`. Do not hand-roll money arithmetic and never use floating-point percentages. Respect quantity semantics: **flat fees are charged once per line**, per-unit multiplies, volume prices the whole quantity at the reached tier, graduated prices each band.\n4. **Never combine unlike periods.** Persist and display the one-time total plus one total per `(currency, interval, intervalCount)`. Do not derive ARR/MRR/TCV — contract term is not modeled.\n5. Guard draft edits with `expectedRevision` (optimistic concurrency) and gate every action with `fromStates`. A quote binds one price book and one currency; a mismatched, inactive, incomplete or quote-ineligible offer is refused. A draft line re-prices only from its pinned offer revision.\n6. Never delete records: soft-remove draft lines.\n\n## Create a discount policy version\n\n1. `{ name, version, label, config, evaluate(context) }` — `evaluate` gets a deep-frozen context (totals, `maxLineDiscountBps`, `effectiveDiscountBps`, lines, opportunity evidence, actor, frozen `config`) and must return **synchronously** `{ decision: 'auto_approve'|'approval_required'|'reject', reason?, requiredApprovalKey?, matchedRule? }`. Promises, network calls, LLM calls and DB writes are forbidden.\n2. **Declare every threshold in `config`.** The fingerprint is a *declared-definition* fingerprint: closure-held values are invisible to it. A closure threshold changes silently — put it in `config`.\n3. **Never edit a registered version.** Changing rules or config = a NEW version registered alongside the old (the persisted fingerprint check stops the app). Rollback = publish a new version derived from an earlier one.\n4. Test both branches end to end: a low discount auto-approves with no approval record; a high discount creates exactly one approval and parks the quote in `pending_approval`.\n\n## Human approval boundary\n\nOnly `actor.type === 'user'` may approve or reject (agent actors get `403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`), one decision per version, decision + quote lifecycle committed atomically. `requiredApprovalKey` is a **label** — never claim Sales-Manager or Finance role enforcement before the Production Spine (auth, tenancy, RBAC).\n\n## Do not implement here\n\nSignature, envelopes, signed artifacts, Orders, taxes, currency conversion, ISO exponent handling, metered usage, overage, proration, ramps, minimum commitments, attribute-based pricing, quote PDFs, payment, billing — Signature and Order are Milestone 11; the rest is later CPQ work. Volume and graduated tiering ARE supported (M10); usage-based rating is not.\n\nFinish with `npm run verify` and the starter (`node examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/install.mjs`).\n","numLines":59,"startLine":1,"totalLines":59}}}

This is the mechanism: it registers versioned, fingerprinted **discount policy** definitions (and catalog providers), validates their shape, and refuses boot if a registered version's source/config drifts (ADR-015 declared-definition fingerprinting).\n- `packages/core/src/commercial-money.js` — safe-integer money/discount arithmetic (basis points, tier calc, rounding rules). Never hand-roll this.\n- `packages/core/src/commercial-actions.js` — the quote actions (`create-quote`, `add-line`, `submit`, `approve`, `reject`, `revise`) that call the policy and enforce lifecycle.\n- `packages/commercial/generated/index.js` — the ten read-only, all-managed storage modules (product, offer, quote, quote-version, quote-approval, etc.) generated on the standard M9 module machinery.\n- `examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/commercial.js` — where an actual, concrete policy is defined: `standard-sales-discount` v1 and v2. synchronously: auto_approve | approval_required | reject\n  },\n};\n```\n\nKey rules already enforced by the framework (not up for negotiation):\n- Every threshold must live in `config` (fingerprinted) — a closure-held threshold is invisible to the fingerprint and \"changes silently,\" so it's rejected in review.\n- You never edit a registered version — a new rule/threshold is a **new version** (`v2` already exists as an example of \"tighter auto-approve\").\n- `evaluate` must be synchronous, pure, no I/O, no LLM calls.\n- The decision + policy name/version/fingerprint are
